original not yet held; replace and re-check quotes when an official copy is retrieved) was read in full. The MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl row with the same sha256 (d10717868f0e...) has the heading 'Executive Order 6530—Code of Fair Competition for the Medium and Low Priced Jewelry Manufacturing Industry', the date 1933-12-23 (which agrees with the date line of the held text), and a text_sha256 equal to the held file's; the row records the common-crawl WARC location. The 1944 WPA numbered list (jobs/x1-class-engine/data/WPA_NUMBERED_ENTRIES.csv) carries the same number and date: 'Code of Fair Competition for the Medium and Low Priced Jewelry Manufacturing Industry approved. IV NRA Codes 355. (Dec 23, 1933)'. Quotations are taken from the registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy. EO 6530 (December 23, 1933) approves the Code of Fair Competition for the Medium and Low Priced Jewelry Manufacturing Industry 'subject to the following condition: That the application of the provision of sub-section (b), Section 1, Article III, which provides for the payment of time and one third for all hours worked by any employee in excess of forty (40) per week, shall be stayed until March 1, 1934, at which time such provision shall become effective and have the same force and effect as any other provision of the Code.' The dossier's single clause (characters 757-1620) holds two commands with different lifetimes, so the inventory is corrected on the registered text into two scopes: the approval sentence (757-1158) and the stay condition (1160-1525); the signature block and Administrator's recommendation that follow are not operative. Stay condition: it ends by its own terms on 1934-03-01, recorded inclusive (the stay runs through that day) under the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling; the words 'at which time such provision shall become effective' support the exclusive reading, under which the stay stops when March 1, 1934 begins and the overtime provision applies that day. The dossier's own-terms record (until_event, 1934-03-01) matches. After the stay the overtime provision is a provision of the code, not a command of this order. No held order extends, shortens or revokes the stay (searches below; the type rule's outlier case of a stay later modified by administrative order cannot be excluded beyond the held sources). Approval: First end: 1935-06-14, the day Senate Joint Resolution 113 (Public Resolution No. 26, 74th Congress, 49 Stat. 375, chapter 246) was approved. Section 2 of the resolution reads: 'All the provisions of Title I of such Act delegating power to the President to approve or prescribe codes of fair competition and providing for the enforcement of such codes are hereby repealed', with one proviso that narrows only the section 5 antitrust exemption. It names no code and no order, so it is not a naming instrument (owner ruling 2026-10-21, unnamed instruments), and under the owner's 2026-10-28 ruling a general rule ends a command only where something shows it extinguished that command. What shows it here is the text of the Act under which the order was made (48 Stat. 195, held): section 3(a) is the power to approve a code; section 3(b) makes the provisions of an approved code 'the standards of fair competition for such trade or industry' and a violation an unfair method of competition; section 3(c) gives the district courts jurisdiction to restrain violations of 'any code of fair competition approved under this title'; and section 10(b) lets the President cancel or modify 'any order, approval, license, rule, or regulation issued under this title', which shows the approval was a continuing act that the Act made binding through sections 3(b) and 3(c). The approval commands nothing of its own beyond that statutory operation, and the resolution repealed the approval power and the enforcement of codes together, with no clause preserving approved codes. The order's only operative act, making the code the enforceable standard, therefore ended on 1935-06-14. Alternatives considered and disclosed. (a) Earlier: Schechter Poultry Corp. v. United States, 295 U.S. 495, decided May 27, 1935 (caption and syllabus held), says at 542 'the code-making authority thus conferred is an unconstitutional delegation of legislative power'. The judgment concerned the Live Poultry Code; it names no other code, and nothing held shows it extinguished this approval on that date (no order of May 27 through June 14 touches any code). If a reviewer holds it ended every approval, the first end would be 1935-05-27; the claim uses June 14 because that is the first date on which held text removes approval and enforcement for all codes. (b) The original expiry of Title I two years after enactment (section 2(c), June 16, 1935) was displaced when the resolution amended section 2(c) to April 1, 1936; it is later than the repeal. (c) Later: EO 7076 (June 15, 1935, held APP text, page record date 1935-06-15; its held transcription omits the signature and date lines) amends all existing Title I executive orders 'so as to continue them in effect and to extend their operation ... until April 1, 1936, so far as consistent with the provisions of the National Industrial Recovery Act as amended and extended', but provides that it 'shall not be construed as an exercise by the President of any authority, or as authorizing any person or agency ... to approve or prescribe codes of fair competition, to provide for the enforcement of such codes'. Because the approval's force came through the provisions the resolution had repealed the day before, continuing it 'so far as consistent' with the Act as amended leaves it no enforceable operation; EO 7076 is therefore recorded here as a disclosed later order and is not treated as an extension or an end. Held EO 7075 (June 15, 1935) likewise refers to the trades 'heretofore subject to codes of fair competition'. (d) A reviewer who reads section 5 as keeping approved codes 'in effect' for antitrust exemption while Title I stood (to April 1, 1936, plus sixty days) could place a later end for that statutory exemption; the exemption is a statutory consequence, not a command of this order, and the resolution's proviso narrowed it to agreements on section 7(a) requirements and on practices already unlawful. That reading would not revive the approval as an enforceable code. Limit: the approved code's own text is not held, so a duration or cancellation term inside it could not be read; no held order, statute, case or index entry shows a code-specific cancellation, amendment, supersession or expiry. Searches run 2026-10-29. (1) A scan of 51,237 txt, jsonl, csv, json and md files under jobs/ (skipping the derivative folders clause-drafts, claim-kit, case-bundles, claim-drafts, x1-class-engine, readiness-tracker and domesday-register, and cache, capture and pdf folders) for the code's title and for citations of this order by number. (2) Every held order text numbered 7060 or higher (APP, Hathi/Commons printed copies, Wikisource, Federal Register, source-ladder) containing the phrase 'codes of fair competition': only EO 7075, EO 7076 and EO 7323 (and an unrelated 1941 board order, EO 9027). (3) The APP index of orders dated 1935-05-15 through 1935-06-25: no order dated 1935-05-27 through 1935-06-14 touches an NRA code; EO 7075 and 7076 (June 15) and EO 7084 and 7085 (June 24, labor boards) are the next NRA orders. (4) The later-events counter-evidence leads file (none for this order). (5) The machine searches current-law:usc and current-law:ecfr (0 hits) and later-events:ofr_catalog (0 hits; its coverage starts in 1937, so it is not relied on alone). Result of (1): hits are only this order's own records and index rows. Limit: the approved code's own text is not held, so a duration or cancellation term inside it could not be read; no held order, statute, case or index entry shows a code-specific cancellation, amendment, supersession or expiry.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Two commands with different ends: the approval (first end 1935-06-14, repeal of code approval and enforcement) and the stay of the time-and-one-third provision (first end 1934-03-01, own terms). The adoption of the Administrator's report and findings is part of the approval. The recitals and the Administrator's recommendation command nothing. The whole-order end is the later of the two, 1935-06-14.\",\"savings_check\":\"The order states no savings. What survives the repeal is statutory and accrued, not a continuing command of the approval: liabilities and rights that accrued while the code was binding (violations before 1935-06-14, and conduct that complied with it), and the section 5 antitrust exemption for action complying with an approved code 'while this title is in effect ... and for sixty days thereafter' (held, 48 Stat. 195), as narrowed by the resolution's proviso. None of these keeps the approval operative after 1935-06-14. Whether pre-repeal violations remained prosecutable turns on a general saving rule that is not held and is not relied on. The overtime provision that took effect on 1934-03-01 is a provision of the code, not a surviving command of this order.\",\"scope_check\":\"Two scopes after the inventory correction: the approval of the code, and the stay condition on the overtime provision of Article III, section 1(b). and text (APP page check, jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md, done 2026-10-29): the registered American Presidency Project transcription (grade mirror_transcription, acceptable_as_original false; original not yet held; replace and re-check quotes when an official copy is retrieved) was read in full. The MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl row with the same sha256 (d10717868f0e...) has the heading 'Executive Order 6530—Code of Fair Competition for the Medium and Low Priced Jewelry Manufacturing Industry', the date 1933-12-23 (which agrees with the date line of the held text), and a text_sha256 equal to the held file's; the row records the common-crawl WARC location. The 1944 WPA numbered list (jobs/x1-class-engine/data/WPA_NUMBERED_ENTRIES.csv) carries the same number and date: 'Code of Fair Competition for the Medium and Low Priced Jewelry Manufacturing Industry approved. IV NRA Codes 355. (Dec 23, 1933)'. Quotations are taken from the registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy. EO 6530 (December 23, 1933) approves the Code of Fair Competition for the Medium and Low Priced Jewelry Manufacturing Industry 'subject to the following condition: That the application of the provision of sub-section (b), Section 1, Article III, which provides for the payment of time and one third for all hours worked by any employee in excess of forty (40) per week, shall be stayed until March 1, 1934, at which time such provision shall become effective and have the same force and effect as any other provision of the Code.' The dossier's single clause (characters 757-1620) holds two commands with different lifetimes, so the inventory is corrected on the registered text into two scopes: the approval sentence (757-1158) and the stay condition (1160-1525); the signature block and Administrator's recommendation that follow are not operative. Stay condition: it ends by its own terms on 1934-03-01, recorded inclusive (the stay runs through that day) under the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling; the words 'at which time such provision shall become effective' support the exclusive reading, under which the stay stops when March 1, 1934 begins and the overtime provision applies that day. The dossier's own-terms record (until_event, 1934-03-01) matches. After the stay the overtime provision is a provision of the code, not a command of this order. No held order extends, shortens or revokes the stay (searches below; the type rule's outlier case of a stay later modified by administrative order cannot be excluded beyond the held sources). Approval: First end: 1935-06-14, the day Senate Joint Resolution 113 (Public Resolution No. 26, 74th Congress, 49 Stat. 375, chapter 246) was approved. Section 2 of the resolution reads: 'All the provisions of Title I of such Act delegating power to the President to approve or prescribe codes of fair competition and providing for the enforcement of such codes are hereby repealed', with one proviso that narrows only the section 5 antitrust exemption. It names no code and no order, so it is not a naming instrument (owner ruling 2026-10-21, unnamed instruments), and under the owner's 2026-10-28 ruling a general rule ends a command only where something shows it extinguished that command. What shows it here is the text of the Act under which the order was made (48 Stat. 195, held): section 3(a) is the power to approve a code; section 3(b) makes the provisions of an approved code 'the standards of fair competition for such trade or industry' and a violation an unfair method of competition; section 3(c) gives the district courts jurisdiction to restrain violations of 'any code of fair competition approved under this title'; and section 10(b) lets the President cancel or modify 'any order, approval, license, rule, or regulation issued under this title', which shows the approval was a continuing act that the Act made binding through sections 3(b) and 3(c). The approval commands nothing of its own beyond that statutory operation, and the resolution repealed the approval power and the enforcement of codes together, with no clause preserving approved codes. The order's only operative act, making the code the enforceable standard, therefore ended on 1935-06-14. Alternatives considered and disclosed. (a) Earlier: Schechter Poultry Corp. v. United States, 295 U.S. 495, decided May 27, 1935 (caption and syllabus held), says at 542 'the code-making authority thus conferred is an unconstitutional delegation of legislative power'. The judgment concerned the Live Poultry Code; it names no other code, and nothing held shows it extinguished this approval on that date (no order of May 27 through June 14 touches any code). If a reviewer holds it ended every approval, the first end would be 1935-05-27; the claim uses June 14 because that is the first date on which held text removes approval and enforcement for all codes. (b) The original expiry of Title I two years after enactment (section 2(c), June 16, 1935) was displaced when the resolution amended section 2(c) to April 1, 1936; it is later than the repeal. (c) Later: EO 7076 (June 15, 1935, held APP text, page record date 1935-06-15; its held transcription omits the signature and date lines) amends all existing Title I executive orders 'so as to continue them in effect and to extend their operation ... until April 1, 1936, so far as consistent with the provisions of the National Industrial Recovery Act as amended and extended', but provides that it 'shall not be construed as an exercise by the President of any authority, or as authorizing any person or agency ... to approve or prescribe codes of fair competition, to provide for the enforcement of such codes'. Because the approval's force came through the provisions the resolution had repealed the day before, continuing it 'so far as consistent' with the Act as amended leaves it no enforceable operation; EO 7076 is therefore recorded here as a disclosed later order and is not treated as an extension or an end. Held EO 7075 (June 15, 1935) likewise refers to the trades 'heretofore subject to codes of fair competition'. (d) A reviewer who reads section 5 as keeping approved codes 'in effect' for antitrust exemption while Title I stood (to April 1, 1936, plus sixty days) could place a later end for that statutory exemption; the exemption is a statutory consequence, not a command of this order, and the resolution's proviso narrowed it to agreements on section 7(a) requirements and on practices already unlawful. That reading would not revive the approval as an enforceable code. Limit: the approved code's own text is not held, so a duration or cancellation term inside it could not be read; no held order, statute, case or index entry shows a code-specific cancellation, amendment, supersession or expiry. Searches run 2026-10-29. (1) A scan of 51,237 txt, jsonl, csv, json and md files under jobs/ (skipping the derivative folders clause-drafts, claim-kit, case-bundles, claim-drafts, x1-class-engine, readiness-tracker and domesday-register, and cache, capture and pdf folders) for the code's title and for citations of this order by number. (2) Every held order text numbered 7060 or higher (APP, Hathi/Commons printed copies, Wikisource, Federal Register, source-ladder) containing the phrase 'codes of fair competition': only EO 7075, EO 7076 and EO 7323 (and an unrelated 1941 board order, EO 9027). (3) The APP index of orders dated 1935-05-15 through 1935-06-25: no order dated 1935-05-27 through 1935-06-14 touches an NRA code; EO 7075 and 7076 (June 15) and EO 7084 and 7085 (June 24, labor boards) are the next NRA orders. (4) The later-events counter-evidence leads file (none for this order). (5) The machine searches current-law:usc and current-law:ecfr (0 hits) and later-events:ofr_catalog (0 hits; its coverage starts in 1937, so it is not relied on alone). Result of (1): hits are only this order's own records and index rows. The phrase 'time and one third' also appears in EO 6393 (Toy and Playthings code) and EO 6450 (Precious Jewelry Producing code), which are other codes and do not mention this order or its stay.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Stay condition: own-terms end 1934-03-01 (inclusive; exclusive alternative stated); no held order mentions the stay or the code after December 23, 1933 (Result of (1): hits are only this order's own records and index rows. The phrase 'time and one third' also appears in EO 6393 (Toy and Playthings code) and EO 6450 (Precious Jewelry Producing code), which are other codes and do not mention this order or its stay.). Approval: own terms none. Alternatives considered and disclosed. (a) Earlier: Schechter Poultry Corp. v. United States, 295 U.S. 495, decided May 27, 1935 (caption and syllabus held), says at 542 'the code-making authority thus conferred is an unconstitutional delegation of legislative power'. The judgment concerned the Live Poultry Code; it names no other code, and nothing held shows it extinguished this approval on that date (no order of May 27 through June 14 touches any code). If a reviewer holds it ended every approval, the first end would be 1935-05-27; the claim uses June 14 because that is the first date on which held text removes approval and enforcement for all codes. (b) The original expiry of Title I two years after enactment (section 2(c), June 16, 1935) was displaced when the resolution amended section 2(c) to April 1, 1936; it is later than the repeal. (c) Later: EO 7076 (June 15, 1935, held APP text, page record date 1935-06-15; its held transcription omits the signature and date lines) amends all existing Title I executive orders 'so as to continue them in effect and to extend their operation ... until April 1, 1936, so far as consistent with the provisions of the National Industrial Recovery Act as amended and extended', but provides that it 'shall not be construed as an exercise by the President of any authority, or as authorizing any person or agency ... to approve or prescribe codes of fair competition, to provide for the enforcement of such codes'. Because the approval's force came through the provisions the resolution had repealed the day before, continuing it 'so far as consistent' with the Act as amended leaves it no enforceable operation; EO 7076 is therefore recorded here as a disclosed later order and is not treated as an extension or an end. Held EO 7075 (June 15, 1935) likewise refers to the trades 'heretofore subject to codes of fair competition'. (d) A reviewer who reads section 5 as keeping approved codes 'in effect' for antitrust exemption while Title I stood (to April 1, 1936, plus sixty days) could place a later end for that statutory exemption; the exemption is a statutory consequence, not a command of this order, and the resolution's proviso narrowed it to agreements on section 7(a) requirements and on practices already unlawful. That reading would not revive the approval as an enforceable code. Limit: the approved code's own text is not held, so a duration or cancellation term inside it could not be read; no held order, statute, case or index entry shows a code-specific cancellation, amendment, supersession or expiry.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Two commands with different ends: the approval (first end 1935-06-14, repeal of code approval and enforcement) and the stay of the time-and-one-third provision (first end 1934-03-01, own terms). The adoption of the Administrator's report and findings is part of the approval. The recitals and the Administrator's recommendation command nothing. The whole-order end is the later of the two, 1935-06-14.\",\"savings_check\":\"The order states no savings. What survives the repeal is statutory and accrued, not a continuing command of the approval: liabilities and rights that accrued while the code was binding (violations before 1935-06-14, and conduct that complied with it), and the section 5 antitrust exemption for action complying with an approved code 'while this title is in effect ... and for sixty days thereafter' (held, 48 Stat. 195), as narrowed by the resolution's proviso. None of these keeps the approval operative after 1935-06-14. Whether pre-repeal violations remained prosecutable turns on a general saving rule that is not held and is not relied on. The overtime provision that took effect on 1934-03-01 is a provision of the code, not a surviving command of this order.\",\"scope_check\":\"Two scopes after the inventory correction: the approval of the code, and the stay condition on the overtime provision of Article III, section 1(b). The stay does not limit the approval's end and the approval's end does not shorten the stay, which had already ended on 1934-03-01. Roosevelt) modifies the Code of Fair Competition for the Baking Industry and the order of May 28, 1934 approving it, under title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act. Read in full in the held American Presidency Project transcription (jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6743-modifying-the-code-fair-competition-for-the-baking-industry-and-the.txt, 1,461 characters, grade mirror_transcription, acceptable_as_original false; the transcription contains optical-character errors such as 'hi' for 'in' and 'tlie' for 'the'). APP page check (AGENT_PROTOCOL.md, working-text paragraph): the jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl row with sha256 60264df5166bf6bd33c72dbf237c46a37988747d9bfc2f264574fc066810fa80 is headed 'Executive Order 6743—Modifying the Code of Fair Competition for the Baking Industry and the Order of Approval Thereof', dated June 16, 1934, node app:node/362518, Common Crawl capture 2025-06-16T14:01:16Z; its text_sha256 bde24fb91473b071f55815d78cfc6157911799116059171c33875ce13083da17 equals the sha256 I computed for the held file, and the page heading, the date and the subject agree with the text. Cotton holds the exact page bytes (jobs/cotton-capture-custody/output/captures). Identity also agrees with the census register row and the WPA 1944 list (page 581: 'Code of Fair Competition for the Baking Industry modified. XII NRA Codes 247.'). The WPA list dates the order June 15, 1934; the page heading, the census register and the held text all say June 16, 1934. The one-day difference does not change identity (number, subject and the NRA Codes citation agree) and enters no end date below, because every end is keyed to the order's own stated July 9, 1934 or to the June 14, 1935 repeal. The order has three numbered operative clauses, which I have read separately: (1) 'the effective date of the Code of Fair Competition for the Baking Industry be and the same hereby is stayed until July 9, 1934'; (2) the second condition of the approving order, which stayed a provision of article VII 'until my further order', 'is hereby eliminated and the provisions of said section shall be ... in full force and effect on the effective date of said code' (the held text reads 'Section G'; the approving order of May 28, 1934, held as a separate APP text, states the stayed provision as 'Section 6', so 'G' is evidently a transcription error for '6'; the lifecycle does not depend on which section); (3) the period in which the Code Authority is to report on the operation and effect of articles III, IV and V 'is hereby extended from the date designated in said order of approval to November 15, 1934'. The machine inventory (three clauses, characters 654 to 789, 791 to 1074 and 1076 to 1426) is complete and correct; the lead-in recital, the 'ORDER NO. 445-2' heading and the signature block command nothing, so no replacement inventory is needed. The dossier's own-terms leads are right for clause 1 (a stated date, July 9, 1934) and clause 3 (a report deadline), and wording matches only for clause 2 ('until my further order' describes the stay that clause 2 removes; clause 2 itself states no period).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Searches run on 2026-10-29 over the held texts (clause-drafts copies excluded): (1) every held American Presidency Project order text numbered 6328 to 7074 (904 files), the government printed copies in jobs/hathi-gpo-orders, the Federal Register texts in jobs/fr-originals, and the other held order text folders, for 'Baking Industry', 'baking', 'bakery' and 'bakers'; (2) a citation search of those folders for this order's number in the forms 'Executive Order', 'E.O.', 'Order No.' and 'Orders Nos.'; (3) a scan of the 904 orders numbered 6328 to 7074 for cancel, revoke, supersede, suspend, rescind and terminate wording in orders that mention a code of fair competition or the National Industrial Recovery Act, which reached section 10(b) cancellation of other codes (for example the order cancelling the approval of the Cinders, Ashes and Scavenger Trade code, December 19, 1934), the service-trades suspension order of May 26, 1934 (which reaches only trades the Administrator later designates, and no held record designates this industry), and stays and terminations of stays in other codes; none names this code or this order. The machine searches of the U.S. Code and eCFR (complete, 0 hits) exclude a current-law reference; the OFR catalog search starts in 1937 and is not relied on alone. The only held orders naming the baking code are the approving order of May 28, 1934 (EO 6725-A, whose government printed copy is not held; the number 6725 in the government printed volume belongs to an unrelated Science Advisory Board order) and this order. No held record shows this code cancelled, modified under section 10(b), superseded, suspended or stayed before June 14, 1935. Later orders that act on Title I generally, of June 15, 1935 (the reorganization of the NRA and the order extending Title I activities to April 1, 1936 'so far as consistent' with Title I as amended, whose proviso disclaims any authority to approve or prescribe codes or to provide for their enforcement), post-date the repeal and do not continue a code approval. Amendments are later events, not ends; June 16, 1935 is not an end, because section 1 of the resolution replaced that expiry with April 1, 1936. Clause 1 has an own-terms end before June 14, 1935 (July 9, 1934), which is its first end. For clauses 2 and 3 no earlier end is held; in particular nothing held modifies, stays or cancels the modification after June 16, 1934.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"None of the three clauses is independent of the code. Clause 1 stays the code's own effective date; clause 2 lifts a condition of the approval of the code; clause 3 adjusts the reporting duty of the Code Authority, a body that exists only under the approved code (the approving order imposes the report). The order makes no appointment, creates no committee or board, delegates nothing under another statute and directs no agency, and no clause here is a stay 'pending further order' (clause 2 removes one), so no independent command is retained and the order is not a mixed order.\",\"savings_check\":\"Clause 1: once the stay ran through July 9, 1934 the code's effective date arrived and the code took effect; that is a completed effect of the stay and keeps nothing of the stay operative. Clauses 2 and 3: Acts done and liabilities incurred under the code before June 14, 1935 are completed (accrued) effects of the approval; the general savings statute of the time, which ordinarily preserves liabilities incurred under a repealed statute, is not a held source, and the repeal text contains no savings clause. Section 5 of title I exempted from the antitrust laws any code approved and in effect under the title and any action complying with it taken during the period; the resolution's proviso limits that exemption to agreements and action thereunder of two stated kinds and states no time limit, so the held text does not settle whether it narrowed the exemption for action taken under codes before June 14, 1935. Whatever exemption or liability survives for past action is an accrued effect of completed acts, not continued operation of this approval. No held source shows a prosecution, injunction or other proceeding under this code continuing after June 14, 1935, and nothing continues the approval itself.\",\"scope_check\":\"Three separate commands, each given its own end: a stay of the code's effective date to a fixed date (clause 1), the removal of a condition that stayed one provision of the approval (clause 2), and the extension of a reporting date for the Code Authority (clause 3). Clause 1 is a stay to a fixed date before June 14, 1935, so its own-terms end controls. The extension in clause 3 moves a deadline: a report deadline is not an end (owner ruling 2026-10-21, report deadlines), so the November 15, 1934 date is recorded as a spent fact, 'functionally spent on November 15, 1934', and the clause's end is the repeal that removed the approval the Code Authority's duty rested on; no held record shows whether the Code Authority reported by that date. No partial event inside any clause is held or found.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"6c0f077c5cc0ffe1f8a172337f76cb19c654217e107501dbf14537f2e93e2694","path":"research/excavator/claude-nira-sweep-2026-10-29/t3/packet/supp/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-nira-sweep-2026-10-29/t3/packet/supp/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:1","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"6c0f077c5cc0ffe1f8a172337f76cb19c654217e107501dbf14537f2e93e2694","path":"research/excavator/claude-nira-sweep-2026-10-29/t3/packet/supp/complete-v2/receipts/research/excavator/claude-nira-sweep-2026-10-29/t3/packet/supp/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:1","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"6c0f077c5cc0ffe1f8a172337f76cb19c654217e107501dbf14537f2e93e2694","path":"research/excavator/claude-nira-sweep-2026-10-29/t3/packet/supp/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:1","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:6743#1"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:6743#1"},{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:6743#2"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:6743#2"},{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:6743#3"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:6743#3"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"bde24fb91473b071f55815d78cfc6157911799116059171c33875ce13083da17","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6743-modifying-the-code-fair-competition-for-the-baking-industry-and-the.txt","sha256":"bde24fb91473b071f55815d78cfc6157911799116059171c33875ce13083da17","state":"match"},"sha256":"60264df5166bf6bd33c72dbf237c46a37988747d9bfc2f264574fc066810fa80","text_path":"jo EO 8300 has one amendment of the first sentence of Rule VII section 2(b). The originals contain no sunset, completed assignment, or independent command outside those host-rule amendments. Probation and objection conditions govern recurring application rather than ending the entire order. EO 8707 and EO 8423 are later amendments, with before-and-after text retained. The earlier ACT commentary that EO 8707 ended EO 8179 item 2 is reconsidered under the owner's October 21 strike-and-insert ruling, which continues the amended clause.\n\nThe full text and operative pages of EO 9830 were examined. Its opening May 1, 1947 effective date governs Parts I, II and III. Part II replaces the Civil Service Rules, and Part III expressly names both target orders and their original dates without a savings qualification. Every original footprint therefore ends on May 1, 1947 under the owner's October 21 unnamed-instruments convention. This is the convention's named endpoint, not a claim that no substantive application changed earlier. The earlier War Service Regulations and 1946 temporary regulations are pinned and expressly disclosed as candidate earlier changes. The 1942 list does not include Rule XI. The 1946 effective-date qualification is preserved rather than converted to an unsupported exact date.\n\nEO 9691 and temporary regulation 27.16(g) protect acquired rights, tenure and benefits. These are separately analyzed as saved acts and successor authority, not silently erased or treated as current authority under the revoked target orders. No independent target command or present application of the old target terms was identified in the bounded later-source, host-rule, savings and revival checks. The U.S. Code machine search reaches October 9 only; it is not the sole bridge to the October 14, 2026 cutoff. This is a producer proposal under the existing codebook and owner rulings, subject to independent whole-case review and human review of the disclosed earlier candidates.\n\nCorrection after independent review: EO8514 is a distinct earlier exception affecting item2. It permits Commission-approved probationary transfers into positions directly concerned with national defense without a six-month minimum. EO8707 does not subsume it. The authorization begins on August13,1940 under its immediate operative wording and is expressly revoked by EO9691 section9 on February4,1946. The acquired-reemployment-rights saving is retained without assuming all rights were extinguished or all other wartime limitations were removed. This member-specific exception and its end are pinned in the actual item2 later events and earlier-candidate disclosure; the May1,1947 named target endpoint remains unchanged under the owner fields which are important in rela­\ntion to production costs or the cost of \nliving in which in their judgment the \ncontrols have heretofore been insuffi­\nciently effective. ..\nrv\n1.","With any \nproper suit, action, or other proceeding \nWith respect to any such-violation, right, \nliability, or appeal.","Department Circular No. 653, Second \nRevision, dated August 31, 1943, as sup­\nplemented June 7, 1944, fixing the terms \nand governing the issue of United States \nSavings Bonds of Series E, currently \ndesignated War Savings Bonds, is hereby \nfurther supplemented, as follows:\n1 .","The provisions of \nsaid Circular No. 653, Second Revision, \nand the regulations governing savings \nbonds shall extend to bonds of Series E \nin the denomination of $200.\n2.","Such termination on October  \n1, 1945 of the order and directions referred  \nto above shall not affect any liabilities in­\ncurred for violation of any of such orders or  \ndirections, or of actions taken by the War  \nProduction Board under them."],"scope":"partial","signing_date":"1945-08-18","target_id":"EO:9301","verb":"revoke"}

  Text: [Tc0d25f1ea252eaf75808df75](#tc0d25f1ea252eaf75808df75); absolute characters 44378–44738
- **ACT:EO:9301>EO:9600@860a9be75cf1c7b7**; Scad55c52937ccfc27722d6bb; {"actor_id":"EO:9600","char_end":90510,"char_start":89760,"confidence":"medium","effective_class":"agency_variable","effective_date":null,"effective_wording":[{"char_end":2784,"char_start":2309,"text":"To assist in the maximum produc­\ntion of goods and services required to \nmeet domestic and foreign needs, (1) by \nassuring assistance in making available \nmaterials and supplies required for the \nproduction of such goods and services;\n(2) by providing assistance to the con­\nversion and utilization of war plants and \nfacilities, both privately and publicly \nowned; and (3) by providing effective \njob placement assistance to war workers \nand returning service men and women."},{"char_end":4492,"char_start":3495,"text":"The departments and agencies of \nthe Government shall take vigorous, con­\ncerted and uniform action toward these \nends and pursuant to this Order, under \nthe guidance and direction of the Direc­\ntor of War Mobilization and Recon­\nversion.\nn\nDuring the transition to a free econ­\nomy, the Secretary of Agriculture, the \nFederal Loan Administrator, and the Di­\nrector of Economic Stabilization shall \nnot only take all measures required by \nlaw to support prices but shall take such \nfurther measures authorized by law as \nmay be necessary to prevent any collapse \nof values or discouragement of the full \nand effective use of productive resources.\nn i\nThe Price Administrator, and in the \nexercise of his price responsibilities un­\nder the law the Secretary of Agriculture, \nshall, subject to such directives provided \nfor by law as may be issued by the Eco­\nnomic Stabilization Director, take all \nnecessary steps to assure that the cost \nof living and the general level of prices \nshall not rise."},{"char_end":4882,"char_start":4493,"text":"Subject to such author­\nity, the Price Administrator and, in the \nexercise of his price responsibilities un­\nder the law, the Secretary of Agricul­\nture, are authorized to make such adjust­\nments in existing price controls as are \nnecessary to remove gross inequities or to \ncorrect maladjustments or inequities \nwhich would interfere with the effective \ntransition to a peacetime economy."},{"char_end":23412,"char_start":21513,"text":"Part 907—Governing employ­\nment stabilization pro*\ngrams__________________- 10106\nChapter IX —Agriculture De­\npartment (Agricultural La­\nbor) :\nPart 1104—Salaries a n d\nwages, South Dakota ____10166\nT it l e 31—M o n e y and  F in a n c e:\nT r easur y :\nChapter II—Fiscal Service:\nPart 316—Offering of War\nSavings Bonds, Series E. 10166 \nT it l e 32—N atio nal  D e fe nse :\nChapter IX —War Production \nBoard:\nPart 944—Priorities system \noperating regulations (2\ndocuments) _______10174,10179\nChapter X I—Office of Price \nAdministration:\nPart 1300—Procedure________ 10226\nT itle 43— P ublic  L ands: I nte ­\nrior:\nChapter I—General Land Of­\nfice:\nPart 115—Revested Oregon \nand California Railroad \nand reconveyed Coos Bay \nWagon Road grant lands\nin Oregon ______________ 10233\nTitle 49—T ransportation ^  and \nRailroads :\nChapter I—Interstate Com­\nmerce Commission:\nPart 405—Surety bonds and\npolicies of insurance ____ 10235\nChapter II—Office of Defense \nTransportation:\nPart 500—Conservation of\nrail equipment _________ 10235\nPart 501—-Conservation of \nmotor  equipment (16\ndocuments) _______ 10236, 10237\nPart 502—Direction of traf­\nfic movement (3 docu­\nments) -------------- 10236, 10237\nPart 503—Administration (6\ndocuments) ____________ 10238\nT itle  50—W ildlife :\nChapter IV—Office of Coordi­\nnator of Fisheries:\nPart 401—Production of fish­\nery commodities or prod­\nucts------------------------- 10239\nAdministrator and the Secretary of Agri­\nculture respectively shall (1) so far as is \n'reasonable, practicable and necessary for \nthis purpose, see that such price increases \ndo not cause price increases at later levels \nof production or distribution, and (2) \nimprove and tighten price controls in \nthose fields which are important in rela­\ntion to production costs or the cost of \nliving in which in their judgment the \ncontrols have heretofore been insuffi­\nciently effective. ..\nrv\n1."},{"char_end":24968,"char_start":24288,"text":"In addition to the authority to ap­\nprove increases to correct gross inequi­\nties and for other specified purposes, \nconferred by Section 2 of Title n  \nof Executive Order 9250, the National \nWar Labor Board or other designated \nagency is hereby authorized to approve, \nwithout regard to the limitations con­\ntained in any other orders or directives, \nsuch increases as may be necessary to \ncorrect maladjustments or inequities \nwhich would interfere with the effective \ntransition to a peacetime economy; pro­\nvided, however, that in /  dispute cases \nthis additional authority shall not be \nused to direct increases to be effective as \nof a date prior to the date of this order."},{"char_end":25452,"char_start":24969,"text":"Where the National War Labor \nBoard or other designated agency, or the \nPrice Administrator, shall have reason to \nbelieve that a proposed wage or salary \nincrease will require a change in the \nprice ceiling of the commodity or serv­\nices involved, such proposed increase, if \napproved by the National War Labor \nBoard or such other designated agency \nunder the authority of this section shall \nbecome effective only if also approved by \nthe Director of Economic Stabilization.\n/ 3 ."},{"char_end":26120,"char_start":25453,"text":"Officials charged with the settle­\nment of labor disputes in accordance \nwith the terms of Executive Order 9017 \nand Section 7 of the,War Labor Disputes \nAct shall consider that labor disputes \nwhich would interrupt work contribut­\ning to the production of military supplies \nor interfere with effective transition to \na peacetime economy are disputes which \ninterrupt work contributing to the effec­\ntive prosecution of the war.\nfacilitate the fulfillment of relief and \nother essential export programs; and \nallocate scarce materials or facilities \nnecessary for the production of low- \npriced items essential to the continued \nsuccess of the stabilization program."},{"char_end":35852,"char_start":35364,"text":"Applica­\ntions shall be submitted to the office of \nthe county association on or before the \napplicable closing date shown in §  415.93.\n§ 415.53 Acceptance of applications by  \nthe Corporation,  (a) Upon acceptance \nof an application by a duly authorized \nrepresentative of the Corporation the in­\nsurance contract shall be in effect, pro­\nvided such application is submitted in \naccordance with the provisions of the \napplication and of this subpart, includ­\ning any amendments thereto."},{"char_end":38766,"char_start":38447,"text":"Insurance shall cease with respect to \nany portion of the flax crop covered by \nthe insurance contract upon threshing \nor removal from the farm, but in no \nevent shall the insurance remain in \neffect later than October 31, 1946, unless \nsuch time is extended in writing by the \nCorporation.\n§ 415.57 Insured production."},{"char_end":44738,"char_start":44378,"text":"Where any such deduction is \nmade before the date of maturity, the \ncash equivalent of the deduction will be \nbased on the Cash equivalent price used \nin computing the indemnity payment or \nthe cash equivalent price determined by \nthe Corporation to be in effect on the \nday the county committee approves such \nloan or other payment, whichever is ap­\nplicable."},{"char_end":84470,"char_start":84393,"text":"This order shall become effective at \n12:01 a. m., e. w. t., August 19, 1945."},{"char_end":87410,"char_start":87334,"text":"This order shall become effective at \n12:01 a. m., e. w. t., August T9,1945."},{"char_end":88381,"char_start":88290,"text":"Effective August 14, 1945, Part 61 of \nthe Civil Air Regulations is amended as \nfollows:\n1."},{"char_end":89448,"char_start":89399,"text":"This regulation shall be effective im­\nmediately."},{"char_end":90944,"char_start":90659,"text":"Part 907, §§ 907.1 to 907.8, inclusive, \neffective August 16,1943, entitled “Regu­\nlation No. 7 (8 F.R. 11338, 9 F.R. 5400, \n12917, 10 F.R. 235, 9522) Governing \nEmployment Stabilization Programs,” as \namended, and all employment stabiliza­\ntion programs promulgated pursuant \nthereto."},{"char_end":94814,"char_start":94795,"text":"(e) Effective date."},{"char_end":117861,"char_start":117606,"text":"Shipments of such commod­\nities moving to a vessel subsequent to the \neffective date of this amendment pursu­\nant to Office of Defense Transportation \npermits issued prior to such date may \nalso be exported under the previous gen-\neral license provisions."},{"char_end":118117,"char_start":117862,"text":"Shipments of any \nof the above commodities whose G-Post \ndollar value limits have been reduced \nand which were mailed prior to the, ef­\nfective date of this amendment may also \nbe exported under the G-Post general \nlicense provisions previously in effect."},{"char_end":118464,"char_start":118118,"text":"This amendment shall become effective \nimmediately upon publication, except \nthat with respect to the commodities \n“Bismuth salts and compounds, medici­\nnal grade, other” and “Pharmaceutical \ndextrose and glucose including dextrose- \nmonohydrate” it shall become effective \non August 21, 1945.\n10168 FED ER A L REGISTER, Tuesday, August 21, 1945_"},{"char_end":119985,"char_start":119670,"text":"No. Commodity\n915800____Surgical appliances (include ar­\ntificial 1  i m b s, crutches,  \ntrusses, invalids’ and wheel  \nchairs, and hearing devices)\nis amended to read: \\\n915800------Surgical appliances except elastic\ntrusses and hearing devices\nThis amendment shall become effective \nimmediately upon publication."},{"char_end":125854,"char_start":125802,"text":"(e) This order shall take effect on \nAugust 18,1945."},{"char_end":129392,"char_start":129270,"text":"This does not apply \nto material already delivered or in transit \nfor delivery to it on the effective date of \nthis order."},{"char_end":130034,"char_start":129980,"text":"(f) This order shall take effect on Au­\ngust 18, 1945."},{"char_end":132298,"char_start":131988,"text":"White and Ruth White shall not \nfor a follows:\n1 .","The provisions of \nsaid Circular No. 653, Second Revision, \nand the regulations governing savings \nbonds shall extend to bonds of Series E \nin the denomination of $200.\n2.","Such termination on October  \n1, 1945 of the order and directions referred  \nto above shall not affect any liabilities in­\ncurred for violation of any of such orders or  \ndirections, or of actions taken by the War  \nProduction Board under them."],"scope":"partial","signing_date":"1945-08-18","target_id":"EO:9301","verb":"revoke"}

check independent commands and surviving effects.","Investigate cessation before the later revocation/ending instrument; locating that instrument does not establish the first end."],"scope_id":"EO:9301#5","state":"requires_clause_specific_research"}],"existing_lifecycle_questions_notice":"Current dossier research prompts, not new gap findings or overrides of prior claim/review conclusions.","new_legal_gaps_invented":[],"prior_differences_require_scope_source_proposition_review":true},"selection_and_checkpoint_records":[],"state":"prior_work_located","status_holds":[],"status_research":[],"unattached_status_reviews":[],"unfinished_work":[],"versions":[{"claim_sha256":"d5dd2e6644f456a5afd6a21a237a6e9c1a8fe069f865ab729ecbaadb75a2ced9","current_canonical":true,"locations":[{"file_sha256":"e9b3cf573404777c34fa056f3bec9469aa69a7ac5f1f9f9849b16da2c2e0d7a1","path":"research/readiness/claims/claude-2026-11-04-tribonian-a01.jsonl","pointer":"line:3"}],"reported_material_gaps":[],"research_rationale":"EO 9301 (February 9, 1943, Franklin D. Roosevelt; 8 FR 1825, issue of Thursday, February 11, 1943), read in full in the held Federal Register text, declares that \"For the duration of the war\" no plant or place of employment is deemed to make the most effective use of manpower if its minimum workweek is under 48 hours (section 1, inside the lead row of the machine inventory), directs agencies to require contractors to comply (2), authorizes the Chairman of the War Manpower Commission to interpret the order and issue policies, directives and regulations and to set a different workweek in particular cases (3), requires agencies to comply (4) and states that the order is not to supersede other hours or wage laws (5). EO 9607 (August 30, 1945), held Federal Register text, revokes it by number in one sentence. The phrase \"For the duration of the war\" is an own-terms condition; no held instrument or source fixes a legal end of the war before August 30, 1945, and EO 9607 revokes the order outright without reciting that it had lapsed, so the first end is the express revocation.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier end is held. Own terms: section 1 begins \\\"For the duration of the war\\\", which ends on a legal event that no held source places before August 30, 1945 (the formal proclamation of the war's end is not held), so it is a disclosed candidate that cannot be dated and not an end. A later general rule is not shown to have ended any command. Searches of the held corpus for \\\"Executive Order 9301\\\" and for the minimum-workweek wording found EO 9607 (the revocation) and EO 9332 (Solid Fuels Administration), which cites EO 9301 in a list of orders and does not revoke or amend it. The contents table of the held Federal Register issue of August 21, 1945 (10 FR page 10166) lists a rescission of the War Manpower Commission's regulations on the minimum wartime workweek; that page is not held, so the rescission's date and text cannot be read. It would concern regulations issued under section 3, not the order, and it does not name EO 9301's revocation, so it is a disclosed earlier candidate and not an end.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Section 3 gives the Chairman of the War Manpower Commission authority to issue regulations and set a different workweek; it is a delegation inside the order and ends with it. Section 5 is a rule of construction protecting other laws and agreements and has no force once the order is revoked. No other independent command is found.\",\"savings_check\":\"EO 9607 consists of the revocation sentence and no saving, exception or transition wording. Obligations that contractors incurred under contracts made while EO 9301 and the War Manpower Commission's regulations were in force arise from those contracts and the regulations, not from a continuing command of the order, and no held source shows any continuing effect; that is a disclosed qualification, not a finding.\",\"scope_check\":\"The inventory is five rows: the lead (authority recital and section 1) and sections 2 to 5. All rows end together on the revocation of the whole order; no row was amended or ended separately before then.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:9301#lead"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:9301#lead"},{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:9301#2"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:9301#2"},{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:9301#3"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:9301#3"},{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:9301#4"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:9301#4"},{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:9301#5"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:9301#5"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"5024ece811adc2ec6fab3ea8f79d9db5df37727da29731456915afac97ee9cbb","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_9607.txt","sha256":"5024ece811adc2ec6fab3ea8f79d9db5df37727da29731456915afac97ee9cbb","state":"match"},"sha256":"8a2a2236dbeeebead3a1f839726e6f95e7afffc0bb3bd9669038bd683f592dc3","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_9607.txt","text_sha256":"5024ece811adc2ec6fab3ea8f79d9db5df37727da29731456915afac97ee9cbb"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"0fa39031e3bb77c3277d25fddd9b4a8e5a6dc6f1b6c43b12c2de9f41e923b67f","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_9301.txt","sha256":"0fa39031e3bb77c3277d25fddd9b4a8e5a6dc6f1b6c43b12c2de9f41e923b67f","state":"match"},"sha256":"06b7cfe79a024de1d4e6d8d2a91cc4ebe64cfbc5686c484448f58780e821b717","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_9301.txt","text_sha256":"0fa39031e3bb77c3277d25fddd9b4a8e5a6dc6f1b6c43b12c2de9f41e923b67f"}]}]}

Needed: the Department of State regulations issued under EOs 9452 and 9521 (22 CFR parts 101-105, 1945-1957) that name this order, or proof that none did; then the first end is either that regulation's issuance date or 1957-03-01."],"research_rationale":"EO 9303 (Feb. 11, 1943; 8 FR 1911) has three operative paragraphs amending the Foreign Service Regulations of EO 7968: para. 1 rewrites item 30 of the Tariff of Foreign Service Fees (no fee for notarial and other services to foreign officials who give reciprocal courtesies); para. 2 revokes secs. V-49 to V-52; para. 3 deletes 'in the handwriting of the officer' from sec. V-54(d). The 1989 Codification gives 'Revoked by EOs 9521, 10697'. EO 9521 revoked the listed orders only as of the issuance of Secretary of State regulations superseding them, and EO 9591 (1945) and EO 9767 (1946) still describe the Tariff as amended by this order, so EO 9521 had not operated on the tariff item by 1946; EO 10697 sec. 7 revoked whatever remained, effective March 1, 1957. Every clause is terminated by 1957 at the latest, but which date ends each paragraph depends on Secretary of State regulations not held, so first_end is left open. The para. 1 table is printed with the item number misread as '80' in the OCR. no held source shows whether V-23 was still in force on March 1, 1957, so first_end and whole_order_end are left open."],"research_rationale":"EO 9591 of July 21, 1945 (official text 10 FR 9205, saved OCR text layer of the govinfo issue PDF read in full). The OCR interleaves columns: the order's heading, enacting clause and paragraphs 1-2 run continuously, then text of EO 9592 and a Civil Service Commission rule intervene before the signature block 'July 21,1945' with F.R. Doc. 45-13508 (EO 9592 carries 45-13509 and its own signature), so the order consists of paragraphs 1 and 2 only. Paragraph 1's opening words are quoted up to the parenthetical citation because the OCR fuses footnote markers to the cited order numbers there (the fused figures are not valid order numbers); the parenthetical cites EO 7968 as amended by EOs 8297, 9303, 9407 and 9507. Paragraph 1 amends Tariff Items 6, 8 and 34; paragraph 2 amends section V-23 of the Foreign Service Regulations. EO 9767 (1946) and EO 10473 (1953) changed other sub-items of Item 6 but not the eighth heading 'Issue of card or certificate of identity and registration' (EO 10473 inserts a new item immediately below it, so it still stood in 1953), Item 8 or Item 34; EO 10639 (1955) deleted only Items 1-5. EO 10697 (22 FR 777, read in full) prescribed a complete new Tariff effective March 1, 1957 and in section 7 revoked EO 9591, so paragraph 1's clauses first ended 1957-03-01. Paragraph 2 is left open (see the gap): its host section V-23 may have lapsed earlier. This is a partial claim: original text, clause inventory, savings and later-source search are asserted; the ending is not.","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:9591#p1-intro-amends-tariff"},{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:9591#p1a-item6-eighth-heading-identity-card"},{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:9591#p1b-item8-revoked"},{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:9591#p1c-item34-official-forms"},{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:9591#p2-intro-and-a-section-V23-disbursing-agents"},{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:9591#p2b-section-V23-subdivision-b-revoked"}],"source_pins":[]}]}

## Valla review leads and component attestations

Review lead only. Every case needs separate applicability, earlier-ending, scope, independent-command, savings and independent whole-case review.
Preparation attestations never approve legal conclusions or replace whole-case review.
